---
name: code-review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

On top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below — a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:

- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.
- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic ("possible Feature Envy"), never a hard violation — and, like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.

Each smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:

- **Mysterious Name** — a function, variable, or type whose name doesn't reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design's murky.
- **Duplicated Code** — the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.
- **Feature Envy** — a method that reaches into another object's data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.
- **Data Clumps** — the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.
- **Primitive Obsession** — a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.
- **Repeated Switches** — the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.
- **Shotgun Surgery** — one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.
- **Divergent Change** — one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.
- **Speculative Generality** — abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn't have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.
- **Message Chains** — long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn't depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.
- **Middle Man** — a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.
- **Refused Bequest** — a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full — the sub-agent has no other access to it.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls — documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this is

An open-ended artificial-life ecology on WebGPU: closed in matter, open in energy, exact integer physics. Individuals, reproduction and heredity are *inferred* from local rules, never declared (no `Organism` class, no `reproduce()`). Research goal and milestones (M3 bootstrap → M4/M5 ensembles): `docs/plan.md`. Rules in prose plus the arithmetic-bounds argument: `docs/rules.md`.

The repo is a pnpm workspace (TypeScript packages + Vite lab), a Deno toolchain for headless GPU runs, and a Phoenix app (`apps/coordinator`). It is jj-colocated with git (`.jj/`).

## Commands

```bash
pnpm install
pnpm dev                 # lab at http://localhost:5173 (/selftest.html, /island.html)
bin/dev                  # lab + coordinator together (PORT, LAB_PORT override 4000/5173)
pnpm typecheck           # tsc over packages/*/src, apps/lab/src, tests (excludes tests/deno)
pnpm test                # vitest: apps/lab/test, packages/*/test, experiments/test, tools/test
pnpm vitest run packages/sim-ref/test/ref.test.ts -t "<name>"   # single test
deno run -A tests/deno/gpu_golden.ts    # GPU vs CPU reference, bit for bit (native WebGPU)
npx playwright test      # browser golden (tests/e2e), Chrome with WebGPU flags; BL_ALL_ENGINES=1 adds firefox/webkit
BL_E2E_PORT=5241 npx playwright test   # a second worktree on its own port (default 5174; strictPort, never reuses a server)
cd apps/coordinator && mix test         # or `mix test test/coordinator/queue_test.exs`
cd apps/coordinator && mix precommit    # warnings-as-errors, format, test
pnpm gen:prereg          # regenerate experiments/preregistration.md generated sections
pnpm gen:cards           # redraw the link-preview cards (site + one per world) with Chrome/WebGPU; then look at them
```

Run `pnpm install` before any `deno run` in a fresh checkout (deno 2.9 otherwise writes `workspaces` into package.json). Tests that start deno pass `--no-lock` (`tools/test/deno-no-lock.test.ts` checks; a launcher or coordinator a test starts that runs deno itself gets the PATH shim in `tests/deno/lib/no_lock_deno.ts`), but a top-level `deno run`, or a tool that starts deno, still writes a stale deno.lock: when package.json deps change, update it deliberately (`deno cache --frozen tools/stitch.ts` fails while it is stale).

`tests/deno/*.ts` are standalone scripts (not `Deno.test`); run each with `deno run -A tests/deno/<name>.ts`. Several need a real GPU; `coordinator_integration.ts` spawns `mix phx.server` against a scratch data dir.

Experiment CLIs (`tools/`, all Deno, native WebGPU): `run.ts` (write run bundles to `runs/<experiment>/<preset>/<condition>/seed-<n>/`), `analyze.ts` (ensemble analysis), `bootstrap.ts` (M3 MAP-Elites search), `retest.ts`, `calibrate.ts`, `island.ts` (headless island), `stitch.ts` (coordinator segments → bundles). Usage is in each file's header comment and in README.md. `runs/` is gitignored and can hold very large outputs.

## Architecture

**Package imports.** `@bl/*` resolve to `packages/*/src/index.ts` via four separate maps that must stay in sync: `tsconfig.json` paths, `vitest.config.ts` aliases, `deno.json` imports, and `apps/lab/vite.config.ts` aliases. There is no build step for packages.

**Two implementations of one rule set.**
- `packages/sim-ref/src/step.ts` is the executable specification (CPU).
- `packages/sim-gpu/src/shaders.ts` (WGSL) must match it bit for bit. `packages/sim-gpu/src/golden.ts` defines golden cases and runs on any WebGPU host (Deno, browser `/selftest.html`, Playwright).
- `packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts` pins state hashes per `RULE_VERSION` (in `packages/schema/src/config.ts`). Any change to dynamics changes the hashes: an intended rule change bumps `RULE_VERSION` and re-pins deliberately. A change that isn't meant to alter dynamics must leave every pin untouched — new optional `WorldConfig` keys stay absent from `defaultConfig()` so existing configs hash identically.
- All arithmetic is integer (u32 matter, fixed-point controllers, counter-based PRNG keyed on seed/step/cell, stochastic rounding with remainders to the source). Conservation residual is exactly zero. Keep new arithmetic inside the bounds listed in `docs/rules.md` (`MATTER_MAX`, `POOL_MAX`, `mulShr` limits); `validateConfig`/`validateState` enforce them.

**Packages.** `schema` (config, presets, layout, genome, worlds, checkpoint encoding, migration/exchange, M3 founder set) → `sim-ref`, `sim-gpu` → `metrics` (census, tracker, collectives, activity, complexity, stats; runs on readbacks) → `runner` (conditions, `runExperiment`, observers, stitching, island client) and `search` (MAP-Elites, gates). `lineage` (schema, sim-ref) is the lineage inspector's genotype core: keys, ancestry, mutation replay, the controller probe, and the mutation-edge store the lab keeps. `runner` code runs unchanged in browsers and Deno.

**Checkpoints and replay.** A checkpoint artifact carries physics state and observer state together, under one digest. Segmented runs must equal a continuous run byte for byte (`tests/deno/segments.ts`, `tests/deno/stitch.ts`); this determinism is what makes distributed verification possible.

**Archipelago.** `apps/coordinator` (Phoenix, JSON API under `/api`, static status page `priv/static/index.html`) hands islands segment tasks. `Coordinator.Queue` does scheduling, `Coordinator.Segment` owns segment state transitions, and `Coordinator.Store` is content-addressed storage for checkpoints (`.blck`) and bundle files. A fraction of segments is re-run by a second island and compared by state hash. Islands are `apps/lab/island.html` (browser) or `tools/island.ts` (Deno), both via `packages/runner/src/island.ts`. `apps/coordinator/AGENTS.md` has Phoenix/Elixir/OTP conventions to follow there; everything between its `usage-rules` markers is generated by `mix usage_rules.sync` (config in `mix.exs`), so rerun that after dependency changes rather than editing the section. `mix usage_rules.search_docs "<term>" -p <pkg>` searches hexdocs for the coordinator's deps.

**Lab.** `apps/lab/src/sim.worker.ts` owns the WebGPU device, frame loop, rendering and OPFS storage. `apps/lab/src/execution.ts` owns census cadence, observer integrity, checkpoint settlement and replay traversal. State-changing messages run through `exclusive()`, which waits for in-flight frame execution; generation identity invalidates stale readbacks.

**Pre-registration.** `experiments/endpoints.ts` is the source of truth for primary endpoints, held-out observables and activity thresholds, and `tools/analyze.ts` executes it. The generated sections of `experiments/preregistration.md` (between `GENERATED` markers) come from `pnpm gen:prereg`, and `experiments/test/prereg-sync.test.ts` fails when they are stale. Edit `endpoints.ts` and regenerate; never hand-edit inside the markers. The doc is frozen (2026-09-27): its SHA-256 is recorded in `experiments/FROZEN`, `pnpm gen:prereg` now refuses to rewrite the generated sections, and every change goes in a dated amendment section.

**Analysis scale.** Run bundles can be hundreds of MB per run (for example `lineages.tsv`). Analysis tools stream per census rather than loading whole tables; keep new analysis code streaming.

## Codex reviews

Reviews by Codex use **Sol 6.1 at High effort**, not Astra (standing instruction from 2026-10-03, replaces the earlier `gpt-6-astra` policy). Run them read-only at phase boundaries and always before committing:

```bash
codex exec -m gpt-6.1-sol -c model_reasoning_effort=high -s read-only -C <checkout> - < prompt.md > review.txt
```

Use `< /dev/null` when the prompt is an argument (otherwise codex waits on stdin) and `-o <file>` for the final message. Historical "Astra review" comments in the code are provenance for past reviews; leave them. Delegated agents and workspaces follow the same rule.

# I06 Construction that opens another niche: first causal rung (slice plan v2, revised after Sol 6.1 xhigh review; see REVIEW.md)

Status: PLAN stage, 2026-11-01. Branch `claude/i06-niche-construction` from `origin/main` dc7dbbde. Sources: "I06" and "Agent execution contract" in `docs/simulation-research-2026-11-01/report.md`; "Conditioned-field opportunity test" in `docs/evolvability-discovery-2026-10-04/DESIGN.md` (and PLAN.md Stage 4b, ADDENDUM item 2); coordinator decisions D1, D7, D12 in `docs/backlog-run-2026-11-01.html` (the task statement also cites D13; the decision log in this checkout ends at D12, so D13 is treated as a coordinator message, not a checked-in decision). Exploratory, CPU only, mutation off, no physics change, no model calls, no GPU, no deploy. Pattern followed: the I14 slice (`docs/predictability-atlas-2026-11-01/`, `tools/predictability/atlas.ts`, `apps/lab/predictability/`).

## 1. Bounded question

In small `sim-ref` worlds with mutation off, does what a builder leaves behind in the field (structural polymer P and the nutrient/waste/energy pattern it shapes) causally raise a receiver's survival and receiver-labelled biomass inventory ("growth" only where flux attribution shows synthesis, 4.4), relative to (i) a nonbuilder history, (ii) a cost-retaining ablation of the builder, (iii) a matched-inventory spatial scramble of the same field, and (iv) the receiver alone at the same inoculum? Every transfer is a budget-audited analytical intervention with an exact conservation residual of zero.

A positive answer is "a dependency edge builder->receiver is supported for this pair, horizon and field". A negative answer ("no causal benefit detected") is a valid result and is reported as such. It does not say the second link is impossible, does not test whether mutations reach the builder or receiver (that is the next rung), and does not test A+B->C.

Rule that decides the edge label is pre-stated in 5, before any scientific run.

## 2. What is built (one PR)

1. **Evidence fixture with a negative case that fools the naive metric** (section 4). Naive metric: "receiver biomass after conditioning exceeds receiver biomass without conditioning". Negative fixture `energy-gift`: a field that differs from the control only by extra stored free energy E (no polymer, no structure). Naive metric reports a large "benefit"; the matched-inventory and inoculum-matched controls reproduce it, so the edge is labelled unsupported. Positive fixture `hand-wall`: a hand-built polymer structure (known history, no builder involved) whose benefit disappears when the same polymer inventory is scrambled, to show the assay can see spatial dependence at all; it is qualified at the frozen scientific scale on dev (G3), not only at unit-test scale. Further fixtures: `null-field` (identical start in both arms, zero effect), `takeover-only` (no synthesis possible, yet receiver-labelled biomass rises by relabelling existing detritus: the naive inventory metric is fooled a second way).
2. **Small CPU path.** An exact transfer module (ledger, relabel, inoculum placement, scramble), four-arm assay runner on `RefSim`, decision function, evidence sealing. Library in `packages/runner/src/niche/` (no new package, so no alias-map edits; the tool resolves `@bl/*` with the same `registerHooks` shim as `tools/predictability/atlas.ts`). Observers are read-only; a plain-vs-instrumented twin test shows the readout does not change physics.
3. **Manifest and machine-readable evidence.** `docs/niche-construction-2026-11-01/evidence.json` and `manifest.json`, `--write` / `--check` / `--replay-check`.
4. **Working local page** `apps/lab/niche/index.html`, kept off the deployed site like `/predictability/`: the arms side by side per candidate, ledgers, effect sizes with intervals, dependency edge drawn supported / unsupported / unavailable, plus the negative fixture panel. Reads `evidence.json` only; runs no simulation.
5. Docs: this PLAN, `REVIEW.md` (Sol dispositions), `EVIDENCE.md` (human result, written after the run).

## 3. Reuse and the honest limits of reuse

- **World, state and ledger:** `defaultConfig`, `allocState`/`buildWorld` helpers, `validateState`, `totalsOf`, `ledgerResidual`, `stateHash` from `@bl/schema`; `RefSim` from `@bl/sim-ref`. Existing sim-ref physics already makes P do something: polymer gates diffusion of A and C (`De = D*gateK/(gateK+P_t+P_n)`), is the substrate of adhesion, and shields from wounds under `injuryShell`. The builder is a carrier of the existing `genes: "ladder"` BUILD bit (`geneMasks`, optional `geneDrive` floor); the nonbuilder is the same genome with only the decomposition bit. Both keys stay out of `defaultConfig()`; no `WorldConfig` key is added at all.
- **Discovery line:** reuse its vocabulary and gate shapes, not its runner. `EVIDENCE_STATUSES` ("supported", "unsupported-within-tested-domain", "invalid-or-incomplete", "unsupported-measurement") label every edge and fixture; `CampaignPurpose` names (`calibration`, `confirmation`) name the dev and fresh splits; `sha256HexJS` from `@bl/schema` hash evidence. `canonicalJSON` there accepts only safe integers (checked), so every sealed number is an integer or a rational `{num,den}` of integers; BigInt values are decimal strings; no floats appear in sealed payloads. Cases are not run through `runCase`/the coordinator: this is a transfer assay between two stages, which `FixtureSpec`/`InitialRecipe` cannot express without a runner change, and a runner change is out of scope. Stated as a limit, with the DESIGN section 2 control list as the checklist (3.3).
- **Founders:** builder and receiver genomes are taken from `M3_FOUNDERS` (committed, `m3-50886563ec90fb39`), by the pre-stated panel rule in 5.1, never by outcome.
- **Not touched:** `vendor/construction-renewal-v1` (D5 pin), `polymerTransport` (exists only in the pinned rule-2 revision, not in `main`), frozen preregistration, `RULE_VERSION`, golden pins, `docs/rules.md` bounds (the module adds no arithmetic to shared sim state; all of it is analytical, integer, outside the step function).
- **Cost-retaining ablation without a rule change.** The pinned in-sim ablation (`polymerTransport: false`) is unavailable on `main`. The ablation arm is analytical: take the builder's field at the end of conditioning, so builder cost (consumed A, spent E, maintenance, genes) is retained, and convert its structural polymer to waste C inside the transfer, with the released energy `(eP-eC)*P` booked as a named analytical heat term `heatAnalytic`. Matter conserved, energy residual still exactly zero including `heatAnalytic`. It tests "does the polymer itself carry the benefit", not "the pinned ablation". Stated limit; the pinned ablation stays a future item.

## 4. Design

### 4.1 Worlds and stages

Single tile, `tileW=tileH=24` (fixed; no 32x32, for the cost budget in G6), `mutRate=0`, `genes:"ladder"`, `kGene`, `geneFlip:0`, `geneDrive` and light/nutrient from dev calibration, `injury*` absent unless calibration shows polymer is otherwise inert (then `injuryPeriod/Radius/Prob/Shell` set identically in all arms; this is an explicit protocol choice, recorded). `T1` and `T2` are each at most 120. Two stages:

- **Stage 1 (conditioning),** `T1` steps from a **declared total budget** `(matter0, energy0)`: nutrient A everywhere, builder (or nonbuilder) founder cells in a fixed source patch, and a **receiver reserve** `R = (B_r, E_r)` carried off-lattice from step 0 and inert during Stage 1. Accounting follows `packages/schema/src/accounting.ts`, where bound matter B is valued at `eB` per quantum and A at `eA`: funding the reserve removes `B_r` quanta of lattice A (footprint-free cells, raster order), and removes from lattice E exactly `(eB-eA)*B_r + E_r`, so that `reserveEnergy = eB*B_r + E_r` is paid for exactly. Matter: `lattice matter + B_r` is the same in every arm and in the receiver-alone controls. The audited invariant is **lattice plus reserve plus cumulative analytic heat**, cumulative across both stages: `ledgerResidual({energy: energy0}, state) + reserveEnergy + cumulativeHeatAnalytic === 0n` (the helper itself counts neither external term, so the module wraps it and tests the wrapper). Insufficient lattice A or E to fund the reserve makes the block `invalid-or-incomplete`.
- **Stage 2 (assay),** `T2` steps after the transfer, receiver cells tagged by a reserved lineage-id set disjoint from builder ids (mutation off keeps tags stable). One seed per block gives the sim seed for both stages. **Absolute clock:** every Stage 2 arm and control (including the receiver-alone ones) starts at absolute step `T1` with the same sim seed, so the counter-based PRNG keys and the `(step+1) % injuryPeriod` schedule coincide across arms; receiver-alone worlds are constructed and their step counter set to `T1` rather than run from 0. A test checks that every arm in a block sees the same injury steps and RNG keys.

### 4.2 Arms (per candidate pair and seed block)

Conditioning histories (Stage 1): `builder` (BUILD+decomp), `nonbuilder` (decomp only, same genome weights, same founders, same budget), `ablation` (builder history, polymer converted to C at transfer per 3).

Transfers (Stage 2), applied to each history:

- **(a) co-culture:** receiver inoculum placed, living cells kept.
- **(b) builder-removed whole-field transfer:** living cells relabelled non-living: lineage and genome cleared, B/P/E/A/C/S kept in place (matter and energy unchanged, relabel recorded with before/after totals); then the inoculum is placed.
- **(c) matched-inventory scramble:** (b), then each channel (A,B,C,P,E,S) independently permuted over all lattice cells by a seeded integer permutation that does not depend on the history (same permutation for the same seed). Every channel total is preserved exactly; structure is not. Called "scrambled", never "unconditioned". The scramble also destroys E/B ratios, signal gradients and their alignment with receivers (controller inputs), so (c) alone cannot say that polymer arrangement is the cause. Two attribution reconstructions therefore run on the builder history: **(cP) P-only scramble** (permute only P; every other channel stays in place) and **(cNP) non-P scramble** (permute everything except P). They label the edge's mechanism (see 5.3); they do not gate supported/unsupported.

Controls independent of history:

- **(d0) receiver-alone:** empty lattice with the same total matter/energy budget in uniform A (nothing conditioned), same inoculum.
- **(d1E) energy-matched receiver-alone** and **(d1B) biomass-matched receiver-alone:** (d0) with, separately, (d1E) the receiver reserve free energy raised by `dE = max(0, sum E over region in (b) - sum E over region in (d0))`, or (d1B) the reserve B raised by `dB = max(0, sum (A+B+C+P) bound-matter excess over region)` with its chemical-energy debit `(eB-eA)*dB`; the region is the inoculum footprint **plus a halo of radius `h`** (halo declared in the protocol, so an energy gift in an annulus just outside the footprint is matched). The raise is taken from the lattice (debits recorded, totals unchanged, `validateState` and pool caps checked before writing). Inoculum placement is on empty cells, so the footprint itself has no bound matter by construction and the halo is what makes bound-matter matching non-vacuous. These are the "more initial stored energy / matter explains it" controls (DESIGN section 2 requires biomass and free energy varied separately). The decision uses the stronger: `Y_inoc = max(Y(d1E), Y(d1B))`. This is a screen on resource totals delivered as inoculum, not proof that no spatial resource effect exists; stated as a limit.

Inoculum placement (all arms): a fixed footprint of `Nf` cells (disc around a fixed centre, raster order) chosen before any run. Candidate cells are those with `B=P=0`, no lineage, and E and S below `POOL_MAX` minus the per-cell share in **every** arm of the block; the inoculum `(B_r,E_r)` is split evenly over the first `Nmin..Nf` such cells (remainder to the first cells). Nothing is overwritten: only empty bound-matter cells receive B and E, and if fewer than `Nmin=12` common cells exist the block is `invalid-or-incomplete`, reported, and makes the candidate `invalid-or-incomplete` (blocks are not dropped; see 5.3). Every transferred state passes `validateState`, with pool capacity and combined lattice-plus-reserve matter limits checked before any unsigned-buffer write. The receiver genome is the receiver founder, lineage ids from the reserved set.

### 4.3 Budget audit (`transfer.ts`)

Every step produces a `TransferRecord`: `before` and `after` over **lattice plus reserve** (A,B,C,P,E,S, reserve B and E, matter, energy), `living`, `moved` (relabel counts), `converted` (P->C quanta and `heatAnalytic = (eP-eC)*P`, a named analytical heat term, cumulative), `reserveDebit`, `matterResidual = after.matter - before.matter` and `residual = (after.energy + heatAnalytic) - before.energy`; both must be exactly `0n` (BigInt). Placing the inoculum moves quanta from the reserve onto the lattice, so only the lattice-plus-reserve sum is conserved, never the lattice alone. The cumulative invariant of 4.1 holds at every census of both stages. A nonzero residual, an insufficient debit inventory or a failed `validateState` invalidates the block and fails the test. Starting totals per arm are published in evidence.

### 4.4 Readouts (per arm, per block)

Receiver survival (any receiver-lineage B at T2, and at each census); **receiver-labelled biomass inventory** (receiver-lineage B at T2) as the primary endpoint, named that way because transport assigns one genome to a destination's whole incoming bound mass including non-living material (`step.ts` reintegration), so labelled B can rise by takeover of existing detritus with no synthesis (fixture `takeover-only`). "Growth" is claimed only when flux attribution (below) is available and the synthesis share (PHOTO+GROW) of the receiver's gain is at least half; otherwise the edge text says "inventory benefit, growth not attributed". Builder cost: builder-lineage B, lattice/ledger E and A deltas and cumulative heat from census totals; **`kGene` maintenance is not separable** from catalytic and ordinary maintenance in `react` and is reported `unavailable`, not inferred. Inventories before/after each transfer from the audit record. **Spatial dependence** = (b) minus (c) receiver outcome. **Flux attribution** uses the four `roles` counters (PHOTO, GROW, DECOMP, RESP) read by a read-only observer at **every** Stage 2 step (so sampling is complete); each counter saturates at 65,535 in the reference step, so attribution is `unavailable` for a block if any counter reaches 65,535 at any step, with the reason recorded. There is no `mulShr` clamp flag and the plan does not pretend one exists. Census samples are never integrated as if they were every reaction.

### 4.5 Naive-metric trap and fixtures

Unit-test fixtures use 16x16 worlds with `kernelRadius` 5 (schema validation requires dimensions divisible by eight and `2*kernelRadius+1 <= min(tile)`; 12x12 and the default radius 9 are rejected, checked) and few steps so they stay in unit-test time. The positive control is additionally qualified at the frozen scientific scale (24x24, T2, injury regime) on dev (G3); a pass at unit scale does not qualify the full assay.
- `energy-gift`: pair of fields identical except extra E. Three variants: uniform (scramble leaves it unchanged), inside the footprint (scramble removes it, so the scramble alone would mislabel it "spatial"; d1E catches it), and in an annulus just outside the footprint but inside the halo (the case the original footprint-only matching missed; d1E with the halo catches it). All three must be labelled `unsupported-within-tested-domain` while the naive metric `D_naive` is positive.
- `hand-wall`: two pre-stated hand-built structures, W1 a polymer ring around the footprint (gates A/C diffusion) and W2 a polymer floor under the footprint cells (local-P shield under `injuryShell`, which tests cell-local P after decay and so is not provided by a surrounding ring). Same P inventory in the scrambled comparator. The control passes if either shows spatial dependence (positive paired sign test on dev, same rule as 5.3) in the frozen regime; the one used is recorded. If neither does, the slice stops at "assay insensitive" (G3).
- `takeover-only`: synthesis disabled (PHOTO and GROW zero) and receiver-labelled B still rises from relabelling builder detritus; labelled-inventory endpoint is positive, synthesis share zero, so the edge text may not say "growth".
- `null-field`, `empty-receiver-reserve` (reserve spent twice detected), `overwrite-attempt` (placement on occupied cell refused), `pool-cap` (placement refused when a cell is at `POOL_MAX`, with a validateState-rejecting state never emitted), `underfunded-reserve`.

## 5. Pre-stated rules (frozen before the first fresh-seed run)

### 5.1 Candidates and splits
- **Builder:** the `genes:"ladder"` BUILD carrier of founder cluster in the panel `{0, 4, 9}` (clusters of M3 with distinct controllers; cluster 4 and 9 are also the I05 probe clusters, an overlap that is stated). Panel is fixed by id here, not by outcome. **Receiver:** the cluster-12 founder (no BUILD gene), a different controller family. 3 builder candidates x 1 receiver. If the runtime gate G6 fails, drop candidates in order 9, 4, never seeds; dropping is by this runtime rule only and the dropped candidate is reported as `not run`, never as negative.
- **Dev (`calibration`) seeds:** 4 blocks; **fresh (`confirmation`) seeds:** 8 blocks, disjoint from dev and from every reservation in the machine-readable `experiments/discovery/seed-registry.json` (which holds ranges, including 8,500,001-8,599,999, absent from `docs/plan.md`) and from `docs/plan.md`. The two numeric ranges are added to that registry **before** any run; the complete seed lists and the registry digest are bound into the protocol hash; a test calls `seedCollisions` against the file. Dev outcomes may set `T1`, `T2`, grid size, `geneDrive`, `B_r/E_r`, `delta`, footprint, injury yes/no. Fresh seeds are run once after the protocol hash is written to `manifest.json`; fresh results are reported separately and never used to tune. Candidate-level outcomes on dev are not used to drop a candidate (only runtime does).

### 5.2 Estimands
Primary: `Y = receiver-labelled biomass at T2` (survival indicator reported separately), paired within a block across arms. Estimand for each contrast: the probability over fresh block seeds that the per-block paired difference `D` is strictly positive exceeds 1/2 (a median-positive claim about the block-seed distribution, which is what the exact sign test addresses; no symmetry assumption is needed). Contrasts (integers, per block):
- `D_builder = Y(b | builder) - Y(b | nonbuilder)`
- `D_poly = Y(b | builder) - Y(b | ablation)`
- `D_space = Y(b | builder) - Y(c | builder)`
- `D_inoc = Y(b | builder) - max(Y(d1E), Y(d1B))`
- Attribution only (never gates): `D_spaceP = Y(b | builder) - Y(cP | builder)`, `D_spaceNP = Y(b | builder) - Y(cNP | builder)`.
- `D_naive = Y(b | builder) - Y(d0)` (reported to show what the naive metric says; never gates). Co-culture (a) reported alongside and labelled as co-culture.

### 5.3 Edge label (per candidate, on fresh blocks)
- **Validity:** all 8 assigned fresh blocks must be valid with every ledger exactly zero; a placement or ledger failure makes the candidate `invalid-or-incomplete` (blocks are never dropped, because placement exclusions depend on the conditioned fields and dropping them would select on treatment).
- **Per-contrast test:** exact one-sided sign test on the 8 paired differences, **zeros counted as non-positive** (conservative), `p = P(Binomial(8,1/2) >= k)` with `k` = number of strictly positive blocks: `k=8` gives 1/256, `k=7` gives 9/256. A contrast also needs a median per-block effect of at least `delta`.
- **Candidate p-value:** intersection-union over the four gating contrasts: `p_cand = max` of the four p-values (all four must be significant at the candidate's threshold, no within-candidate correction needed). `delta` and the survival rule below are part of the same conjunction.
- **Across candidates:** Holm over the (up to 3) candidates at alpha 0.05, thresholds 1/60, 1/40, 1/20 for 3 candidates (if candidates were dropped by the runtime rule, over those that ran). With 8 blocks the first rejection needs `k=8` in all four contrasts (1/256 < 1/60; `k=7` gives 9/256, above 1/60 and 1/40, below 1/20). Resolution is thereby stated, not hidden: this design can only certify a large, consistent effect.
- **`delta`:** positive and set on dev only: `delta = max(ceil(0.02*B_r), 1.5 * max|D|)` over a **stage-2 replicate control** (same conditioned field, different Stage 2 PRNG seed, nonbuilder vs nonbuilder and builder vs builder); identical-twin replays always give exactly 0 and serve as correctness tests only, never as noise estimates. Estimating `max|D|` from 4 dev blocks is a stated crude floor.
- **Survival:** the receiver survives in (b) in at least 7 of 8 fresh blocks.
- **Labels:** `supported` iff all of the above hold. Mechanism text: "polymer-arrangement-attributed" only if `D_spaceP` also passes the sign test and delta while `D_spaceNP` does not; "non-polymer field pattern" if the reverse; otherwise "combined conditioned field". `unsupported-within-tested-domain` iff valid, measurement adequate and any gating test fails. `unsupported-measurement` iff the positive control (`hand-wall`) shows no effect in the frozen regime, or the builder produced no polymer at T1 (builder P total at or below the dev replicate floor) on dev. `invalid-or-incomplete` for ledger, placement, validation or funding failures.
- **Panel verdict (three-valued):** any candidate `supported` => "causal benefit detected for pair X within this domain"; "no causal benefit detected" only when every candidate that ran was valid, adequately measured (positive control passed) and `unsupported-within-tested-domain`; any invalid or measurement-limited candidate yields an overall `invalid-or-incomplete` or `unsupported-measurement` verdict that names which candidates are negative and which are not usable.

## 7. Tests (named cases)

`niche-transfer.test.ts`: `relabel keeps every channel total and records living count`; `P-to-C conversion books heatAnalytic and residual is zero`; `reserve debit funds eB*B_r + E_r and arm totals equal at step 0`; `cumulative invariant ledger+reserve+heatAnalytic is 0n across both stages`; `underfunded reserve => invalid-or-incomplete`; `pool-cap refusal and validateState on every transferred state`; `placement refuses occupied cell and reports it`; `placement set is the intersection across arms`; `fewer than Nmin common cells => invalid-or-incomplete`; `nonzero residual throws`.
`niche-arms.test.ts`: `scramble preserves each channel total exactly`; `scramble is identical for the same seed across histories`; `scramble changes structure (adjacent-pair statistic differs)`; `d1E and d1B raise reserve and debit the lattice by the same amounts (with eB-eA chemical debit); halo makes bound-matter matching non-vacuous`; `scramble variants cP and cNP preserve channel totals`; `all arms start at absolute step T1 with equal injury steps and RNG keys`; `all arms in a block start with equal matter and energy`.
`niche-assay.test.ts`: `instrumented twin equals plain twin (stateHash)`; `ledgerResidual is 0n at every census of both stages`; `deterministic replay: same block twice gives equal payload bytes`; `segmented stage 2 (checkpoint round trip at midpoint) equals continuous` (CPU `RefSim` + `encode/decode` checkpoint); `mutation off: no new lineage ids`; `flux attribution unavailable when a role counter reaches 65535`; `observer reads roles every Stage 2 step`.
`niche-decision.test.ts`: exact sign-test p-values for k=6,7,8 of 8 against hand enumeration; zeros counted non-positive; intersection-union max; Holm across 3 candidates and resolution (k=7 fails the first two thresholds); `supported` only when all four contrasts, delta and survival pass; `null-field gives unsupported`; `adequacy failure => unsupported-measurement, not unsupported`; `delta is positive and derived from the stage-2 replicate control, never from identical twins`; `panel verdict is not negative when a candidate is invalid`; `no floats in sealed payloads`.
`niche-fixtures.test.ts`: `energy-gift: naive metric positive, edge unsupported (uniform, footprint and annulus-in-halo variants)`; `takeover-only: labelled inventory rises with zero synthesis, edge text omits growth`; `hand-wall: spatial dependence detected, edge supported or measurement-limited as pre-stated`; `null-field zero effect`; `overwrite-attempt refused`.
`niche-evidence.test.ts` (tools/test): `evidence.json is sealed and verifies`; `manifest hashes match sources`; `protocol hash precedes fresh results`; `fresh and dev reported separately`; `seed ranges do not collide with experiments/discovery/seed-registry.json (digest bound in protocol)`; `--check does not simulate`.
Page tests: render fixtures, edge marked supported/unsupported/unavailable, caption "exploratory, CPU only, no GPU evidence", `noindex`, packaging (Vite entry, Dockerfile removal, Caddyfile 404, self-contained asset graph after the removal).

## 8. Acceptance gates (runnable)

```bash
export PATH=~/.nvm/versions/node/v24.2.0/bin:$PATH
pnpm install
pnpm typecheck
pnpm vitest run --maxWorkers=2 packages/runner/test/niche-*.test.ts tools/test/niche-evidence.test.ts apps/lab/test/niche-*.test.ts
node tools/niche/niche.ts --bench                # step rate on this machine, informational
node tools/niche/niche.ts --calibrate            # dev seeds only, writes calibration.json; protocol frozen after this
node tools/niche/niche.ts --write                # once, deliberately, after protocol hash is recorded
node tools/niche/niche.ts --check
node tools/niche/niche.ts --replay-check         # regenerate, byte equality, target under 5 minutes unloaded
pnpm vitest run --maxWorkers=2 packages/sim-ref/test/golden-hashes.test.ts   # pins untouched
git diff --exit-code origin/main -- packages/schema packages/sim-ref packages/sim-gpu vendor experiments/preregistration.md experiments/FROZEN docs/rules.md
test -z "$(git status --porcelain -- packages/schema packages/sim-ref packages/sim-gpu vendor experiments/preregistration.md experiments/FROZEN docs/rules.md)"   # both must succeed
```
Gates: G1 transfer/ledger tests green with zero residual; G2 fixtures behave as stated (negative fooled-then-caught; null zero); G3 dev positive control (`hand-wall`, W1 or W2) shows spatial dependence at the frozen scale/horizon/regime, else the run stops and reports `unsupported-measurement` (assay insensitive) with no fresh run; G4 protocol hash and fresh seeds written before the fresh run; G5 `--check` and `--replay-check` pass; G6 regeneration under 5 minutes. Sizing from Sol's uninstrumented probes (about 3.7 ms/step at 24x24 on Node 24.2): per block 2 Stage 1 histories (the ablation shares the builder history) + 10 Stage 2 runs (builder a,b,c,cP,cNP; nonbuilder b; ablation b; d0,d1E,d1B) = `12*T` steps with `T1=T2=T<=120`; 3 candidates x (8 fresh + 4 dev) = 36 blocks = `432*T` steps, at most 51,840 steps, about 3.2 minutes before observation overhead. `--bench` measures the real rate and the manifest records the projection formula; if the projection exceeds 5 minutes, drop candidates by the runtime rule or lower `T`, never seeds. The original 100,800-step plan (T about 200) was over budget and is replaced. G7 golden pins and both protected-path checks above unchanged, plus rebase onto fresh `origin/main` and focused rechecks before publication (D7); G8 Sol xhigh read-only review of plan (done, dispositions in `REVIEW.md`; raw output in `plan-review-raw.txt`) and of the source before the PR, findings in `REVIEW.md`.

## 9. Non-goals and limits

- No mutation search, no accessibility claim, no A+B->C test, no predation actuator, no organism abstraction, no declared-cell mode.
- No physics or rule change; no new `WorldConfig` key; GPU parity not claimed (CPU only; the transfers are analytical interventions outside the ecology and are not reproduction instructions).
- The pinned `polymerTransport` ablation is not used; the analytical P->C ablation tests a different (narrower) statement. Builder and nonbuilder differ in the BUILD gene and therefore in its maintenance cost; the ablation arm and matched scramble are the controls for that, not the nonbuilder alone.
- Non-living relabelled builder matter (arm b/c) may itself decay or feed the receiver; this is the transformed material and is exactly why (c), (d1E/d1B) and the ablation exist, not a claim that detritus is inert.
- Stage 2 uses one receiver genome and one conditioned field per candidate; a negative bounds this panel, horizon and field only. A candidate showing benefit on dev only is a calibration result, not evidence.
- Eight fresh blocks per candidate with exact sign tests and Holm across candidates can only certify an effect that is positive in all (or nearly all) blocks; a modest real effect will read as unsupported. Stated, and the result is exploratory.
- Small finite worlds (24x24, T at most 120 each): effects that need larger scales or longer horizons are out of reach; reported as such.
- Seeds, scrambles and placement are deterministic; timing numbers are informational and excluded from sealed evidence.
- Everything here is exploratory and not an addition to the frozen preregistration.

     1	// Stage 2 and the readouts. Observers are read-only: they look at sim.state and sim.roles after each step and never write.
     2	import { CH, G, cellCount, packLineageLo, stateHash, totalsOf, validateState, type WorldState } from "@bl/schema";
     3	import { RefSim } from "@bl/sim-ref";
     4	import { buildArms, placeAll, type ArmId, type BlockArms, type PreArm } from "./arms.ts";
     5	import { PROTOCOL, candidateOf, type Protocol } from "./protocol.ts";
     6	import { NicheInvalid, assertAudit, snap, type Snap, type TransferRecord } from "./transfer.ts";
     7	
     8	export interface ArmOutcome {
     9	  id: ArmId;
    10	  /** Receiver-labelled biomass inventory B at the start of Stage 2 and at each census (last entry is T2). */
    11	  receiverB: number[];
    12	  /** Receiver-labelled B at T2: the primary endpoint Y. */
    13	  y: number;
    14	  survived: boolean;
    15	  builderB: number;
    16	  /** Receiver-cell PHOTO + GROW quanta summed over every Stage 2 step (synthesis), and DECOMP, RESP. */
    17	  synthesis: number;
    18	  decomp: number;
    19	  resp: number;
    20	  /** A role counter reached its saturation value at some step: flux attribution is unavailable. */
    21	  saturated: boolean;
    22	  /** Y - B_r gain is at least half synthesis: "growth" may be said. Otherwise "inventory benefit, growth not attributed". */
    23	  growthAttributed: boolean;
    24	  energyEnd: string;
    25	  endHash: string;
    26	}
    27	
    28	export interface Hooks {
    29	  /** Called after each step with the state and the roles buffer (read only). */
    30	  onStep?: (state: WorldState, roles: Uint32Array, step: number) => void;
    31	}
    32	
    33	const lin = (s: WorldState, raw: number) => packLineageLo(s.cfg, raw);
    34	
    35	function receiverB(s: WorldState, raw: number): number {
    36	  const n = cellCount(s.cfg);
    37	  const lo = lin(s, raw);
    38	  let t = 0;
    39	  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) if (s.genome[G.LIN_HI * n + i] === 0 && s.genome[G.LIN_LO * n + i] === lo) t += s.cells[CH.B * n + i];
    40	  return t;
    41	}
    42	
    43	/** Run Stage 2 on a placed arm for T2 steps; throws NicheInvalid on any residual. */
    44	export function runArm(x: PreArm, p: Protocol = PROTOCOL, hooks: Hooks = {}): ArmOutcome {
    45	  const sim = new RefSim(x.state);
    46	  const n = cellCount(x.state.cfg);
    47	  const lo = lin(x.state, p.receiverRaw), blo = lin(x.state, p.builderRaw);
    48	  const y0 = receiverB(sim.state, p.receiverRaw);
    49	  const series = [y0];
    50	  let synthesis = 0, decomp = 0, resp = 0, saturated = false;
    51	  for (let t = 1; t <= p.T2; t++) {
    52	    sim.step();
    53	    const s = sim.state, roles = sim.roles;
    54	    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
    55	      const w0 = roles[2 * i], w1 = roles[2 * i + 1];
    56	      if ((w0 & 0xffff) >= p.roleSaturation || w0 >>> 16 >= p.roleSaturation || (w1 & 0xffff) >= p.roleSaturation || w1 >>> 16 >= p.roleSaturation) saturated = true;
    57	      // Roles are counted on every cell where the builder was removed (only receivers react there; injury may clear a lineage after it synthesized). Co-culture counts by lineage and is approximate under injury.
    58	      if (x.id !== "a" || (s.genome[G.LIN_HI * n + i] === 0 && s.genome[G.LIN_LO * n + i] === lo)) {
    59	        synthesis += (w0 & 0xffff) + (w0 >>> 16);
    60	        decomp += w1 & 0xffff;
    61	        resp += w1 >>> 16;
    62	      }
    63	    }
    64	    hooks.onStep?.(s, roles, s.step);
    65	    if (t % p.census === 0 || t === p.T2) {
    66	      assertAudit(s, x.audit, `stage 2 ${x.id} step ${t}`);
    67	      series.push(receiverB(s, p.receiverRaw));
    68	    }
    69	  }
    70	  const s = sim.state;
    71	  const errs = validateState(s);
    72	  if (errs.length) throw new NicheInvalid(`stage 2 ${x.id}: ${errs[0]}`);
    73	  let builderB = 0;
    74	  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) if (s.genome[G.LIN_HI * n + i] === 0 && s.genome[G.LIN_LO * n + i] === blo) builderB += s.cells[CH.B * n + i];
    75	  const y = series[series.length - 1];
    76	  const gain = y - y0;
    77	  return {
    78	    id: x.id,
    79	    receiverB: series,
    80	    y,
    81	    survived: y > 0,
    82	    builderB,
    83	    synthesis,
    84	    decomp,
    85	    resp,
    86	    saturated,
    87	    growthAttributed: !saturated && gain > 0 && 2 * synthesis >= gain,
    88	    energyEnd: `${totalsOf(s.cfg, s.cells).energy}`,
    89	    endHash: stateHash(s),
    90	  };
    91	}
    92	
    93	export interface HistoryCost {
    94	  kind: "builder" | "nonbuilder";
    95	  B: string;
    96	  P: string;
    97	  E: string;
    98	  A: string;
    99	  C: string;
   100	  lightIn: string;
   101	  heatOut: string;
   102	  builderLineageB: number;
   103	  living: number;
   104	  /** kGene maintenance is not separable from catalytic and ordinary maintenance in react(); stated, not inferred. */
   105	  kGeneMaintenance: "unavailable";
   106	}
   107	
   108	export function historyCost(kind: "builder" | "nonbuilder", s: WorldState, p: Protocol): HistoryCost {
   109	  const t = totalsOf(s.cfg, s.cells);
   110	  const n = cellCount(s.cfg);
   111	  let b = 0, living = 0;
   112	  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
   113	    const hi = s.genome[G.LIN_HI * n + i], lo = s.genome[G.LIN_LO * n + i];
   114	    if ((hi | lo) !== 0) living++;
   115	    if (hi === 0 && lo === lin(s, p.builderRaw)) b += s.cells[CH.B * n + i];
   116	  }
   117	  return { kind, B: `${t.B}`, P: `${t.P}`, E: `${t.E}`, A: `${t.A}`, C: `${t.C}`, lightIn: `${s.lightIn}`, heatOut: `${s.heatOut}`, builderLineageB: b, living, kGeneMaintenance: "unavailable" };
   118	}

   120	export interface BlockResult {
   121	  seed: number;
   122	  cluster: number;
   123	  valid: boolean;
   124	  invalidReason: string | null;
   125	  nPlaced: number;
   126	  dE: number;
   127	  dB: number;
   128	  costs: HistoryCost[];
   129	  startTotals: Record<string, Snap>;
   130	  transfers: Record<string, TransferRecord[]>;
   131	  outcomes: Partial<Record<ArmId, ArmOutcome>>;
   132	  /** Integer contrasts (null when invalid). */
   133	  contrasts: Contrasts | null;
   134	}
   135	
   136	export interface Contrasts {
   137	  builder: number;
   138	  poly: number;
   139	  space: number;
   140	  inoc: number;
   141	  spaceP: number;
   142	  spaceNP: number;
   143	  naive: number;
   144	  /** Stage-2 replicate differences (dev only). */
   145	  replicateBuilder: number | null;
   146	  replicateNonbuilder: number | null;
   147	}
   148	
   149	export function contrastsOf(o: Partial<Record<ArmId, ArmOutcome>>): Contrasts {
   150	  const y = (id: ArmId): number => {
   151	    const v = o[id];
   152	    if (!v) throw new NicheInvalid(`arm ${id} missing`);
   153	    return v.y;
   154	  };
   155	  const yb = y("b");
   156	  return {
   157	    builder: yb - y("nb"),
   158	    poly: yb - y("ab"),
   159	    space: yb - y("c"),
   160	    inoc: yb - Math.max(y("d1E"), y("d1B")),
   161	    spaceP: yb - y("cP"),
   162	    spaceNP: yb - y("cNP"),
   163	    naive: yb - y("d0"),
   164	    replicateBuilder: o.rb ? yb - o.rb.y : null,
   165	    replicateNonbuilder: o.rnb ? y("nb") - o.rnb.y : null,
   166	  };
   167	}
   168	
   169	/** One block: Stage 1 histories, transfers, shared placement, all Stage 2 arms. An invalid block is reported, never dropped. */
   170	export function runBlock(cluster: number, seed: number, p: Protocol = PROTOCOL, replicateSeed?: number, hooks: Hooks = {}): BlockResult {
   171	  const base: BlockResult = { seed, cluster, valid: false, invalidReason: null, nPlaced: 0, dE: 0, dB: 0, costs: [], startTotals: {}, transfers: {}, outcomes: {}, contrasts: null };
   172	  let built: BlockArms | null = null;
   173	  try {
   174	    built = buildArms(candidateOf(cluster, p), seed, p, replicateSeed);
   175	    base.dE = built.dE;
   176	    base.dB = built.dB;
   177	    base.costs = [historyCost("builder", built.histories.builder.state, p), historyCost("nonbuilder", built.histories.nonbuilder.state, p)];
   178	    base.nPlaced = placeAll(built.arms, p).length;
   179	    for (const x of built.arms) {
   180	      base.startTotals[x.id] = snap(x.state, x.audit);
   181	      base.transfers[x.id] = x.records;
   182	    }
   183	    for (const x of built.arms) base.outcomes[x.id] = runArm(x, p, hooks);
   184	    base.contrasts = contrastsOf(base.outcomes);
   185	    base.valid = true;
   186	  } catch (e) {
   187	    if (!(e instanceof NicheInvalid)) throw e;
   188	    base.invalidReason = e.reason;
   189	    if (built) for (const x of built.arms) base.transfers[x.id] ??= x.records;
   190	  }
   191	  return base;
   192	}

     1	// Fixtures: the negative case that fools the naive metric (energy-gift), the positive control (hand-wall), the relabelling trap
     2	// (takeover-only), a null field, and the guard checks. Unit-scale fixtures use 16x16 worlds with kernelRadius 5 (schema validation
     3	// needs dimensions divisible by eight and 2*kernelRadius+1 <= tile); the positive control is also qualified at the frozen scale.
     4	import { CH, cellCount, encodeGenome, founderGenome, lowbias32, packLineageLo, type Genome, type WorldState } from "@bl/schema";
     5	import { disc, footprintCells, matchedControls, outsideRegion, placeAll, receiverAlone, regionCells, startWorld, type ArmId, type Budget, type PreArm } from "./arms.ts";
     6	import { contrastsOf, runArm, type ArmOutcome, type Contrasts } from "./assay.ts";
     7	import { decideCandidates, type CandidateResult } from "./decision.ts";
     8	import { PROTOCOL, candidateOf, founderOf, type Protocol } from "./protocol.ts";

    10	
    11	export const FIXTURE_SEEDS: readonly number[] = [8_600_401, 8_600_402, 8_600_403, 8_600_404, 8_600_405, 8_600_406, 8_600_407, 8_600_408];
    12	
    13	export function fixtureProtocol(): Protocol {
    14	  return { ...PROTOCOL, grid: 16, T1: 0, T2: 40, injury: null, footprint: { x: 8, y: 8, radius: 2 }, halo: 2, nMin: 8, reserve: { B: 480, E: 24 }, nutrient: 100 };
    15	}
    16	
    17	/** Uniform-A / uniform-E budget of a w x w lattice with the reserve inside it (no founders). */
    18	export const uniformBudget = (p: Protocol): Budget => {
    19	  const n = p.grid * p.grid;
    20	  return { matterStart: BigInt(p.nutrient * n), energyStart: BigInt(p.backgroundE * n + 10 * p.reserve.B + p.reserve.E) };
    21	};
    22	
    23	const setChannel = (s: WorldState, ch: number, cells: readonly number[], total: number): void => {
    24	  const n = cellCount(s.cfg), m = cells.length, base = Math.floor(total / m), rem = total % m;
    25	  cells.forEach((i, j) => (s.cells[ch * n + i] += base + (j < rem ? 1 : 0)));
    26	};
    27	
    28	/** Run all pre-inoculum arms of a fixture block through the shared placement and Stage 2. */
    29	export function runPre(arms: PreArm[], p: Protocol, opts: { allowP?: boolean; genome?: Genome } = {}): Partial<Record<ArmId, ArmOutcome>> {
    30	  placeAll(arms, p, opts);
    31	  const out: Partial<Record<ArmId, ArmOutcome>> = {};
    32	  for (const x of arms) out[x.id] = runArm(x, p);
    33	  return out;
    34	}
    35	
    36	/** Controls around a conditioned pre-arm `b` (any hand-built field): scrambles, ablation, receiver-alone and matched-inventory. */
    37	export function controlsAround(b: PreArm, d0: PreArm, seed: number, p: Protocol): PreArm[] {
    38	  const mk = (id: ArmId, ...ops: ((s: WorldState, a: Audit) => unknown)[]): PreArm => {
    39	    const state = cloneState(b.state), audit = cloneAudit(b.audit);
    40	    ops.forEach((f) => f(state, audit));
    41	    return { id, state, audit, records: [] };
    42	  };
    43	  const ALL = [CH.A, CH.B, CH.C, CH.P, CH.E, CH.S];
    44	  const sc = (chs: number[]) => (s: WorldState, a: Audit) => scrambleChannels(s, a, seed, chs, lowbias32);
    45	  const { d1E, d1B } = matchedControls(b, d0, p);
    46	  const nb = { id: "nb" as ArmId, state: cloneState(d0.state), audit: cloneAudit(d0.audit), records: [] };
    47	  return [
    48	    { ...b, id: "b" },
    49	    mk("c", sc(ALL)),
    50	    mk("cP", sc([CH.P])),
    51	    mk("cNP", sc(ALL.filter((c) => c !== CH.P))),
    52	    mk("ab", (s, a) => convertPolymer(s, a)),
    53	    nb,
    54	    d0,
    55	    d1E,
    56	    d1B,
    57	  ];
    58	}
    59	
    60	/** Halo ring cells: in the footprint-plus-halo region but not in the footprint. */
    61	export const regionCellsExcludingFootprint = (p: Protocol): number[] => {
    62	  const fp = new Set(footprintCells(p));
    63	  return regionCells(p).filter((i) => !fp.has(i));
    64	};
    65	
    66	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    67	// energy-gift
    68	
    69	export type GiftVariant = "uniform" | "footprint" | "annulus";
    70	/** Extra stored free energy per variant, sized so the naive metric clears the fixture delta in each. */
    71	export const GIFT: Record<GiftVariant, number> = { uniform: 10000, footprint: 4000, annulus: 4000 };
    72	
    73	/** A world that differs from receiver-alone only by extra stored free energy, declared in its budget (it is a different budget on purpose). */
    74	export function giftBlock(variant: GiftVariant, seed: number, p: Protocol = fixtureProtocol(), gift = GIFT[variant]): { outcomes: Partial<Record<ArmId, ArmOutcome>>; contrasts: Contrasts; gift: number } {
    75	  const cfgSeed = seed;
    76	  const budget = uniformBudget(p);
    77	  const base = receiverAlone({ audit: budget }, cfgSeed, p);
    78	  const cond: PreArm = { id: "b", state: cloneState(base.state), audit: cloneAudit(base.audit), records: [] };
    79	  const W = p.grid;
    80	  const cells = variant === "uniform" ? Array.from({ length: W * W }, (_, i) => i) : variant === "footprint" ? footprintCells(p) : regionCellsExcludingFootprint(p);
    81	  setChannel(cond.state, CH.E, cells, gift);
    82	  cond.audit.energyStart += BigInt(gift);
    83	  const arms = controlsAround(cond, base, seed, p);
    84	  const outcomes = runPre(arms, p);
    85	  return { outcomes, contrasts: contrastsOf(outcomes), gift };
    86	}
    87	
    88	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    89	// hand-wall
    90	
    91	export type WallKind = "W1" | "W2";
    92	
    93	/** W1: a polymer ring around the footprint (gates A/C diffusion). W2: a polymer floor under the footprint cells (a local-P shield under injuryShell). */
    94	export function wallCells(kind: WallKind, p: Protocol): number[] {
    95	  const f = p.footprint;
    96	  if (kind === "W2") return footprintCells(p);
    97	  const inner = new Set(disc(f.x, f.y, f.radius + p.wall.ringGap - 1, p.grid, p.grid));
    98	  return disc(f.x, f.y, f.radius + p.wall.ringGap + 1, p.grid, p.grid).filter((i) => !inner.has(i));
    99	}
   100	
   101	/** Build P on `cells` out of lattice nutrient on the same cells and pay (eP - eA) * P from lattice E outside the region. */
   102	export function buildWall(x: PreArm, cells: readonly number[], totalP: number, p: Protocol): void {
   103	  audited("build-wall", x.state, x.audit, () => {
   104	    const s = x.state;
   105	    debitEven(s, CH.A, totalP, cells);
   106	    setChannel(s, CH.P, cells, totalP);
   107	    debitEven(s, CH.E, (s.cfg.eP - s.cfg.eA) * totalP, outsideRegion(p));
   108	    return { totalP, cells: cells.length };
   109	  });
   110	}
   111	
   112	/** One hand-wall block: the wall against the same P inventory scrambled. D = Y(wall) - Y(P scrambled). */
   113	export function wallBlock(kind: WallKind, seed: number, p: Protocol = PROTOCOL, budget?: Budget): { d: number; wall: ArmOutcome; scrambled: ArmOutcome } {
   114	  const bud = budget ?? nominalBudget(seed, p);
   115	  const base = receiverAlone({ audit: bud }, seed, p);
   116	  const wall: PreArm = { id: "b", state: cloneState(base.state), audit: cloneAudit(base.audit), records: [] };
   117	  buildWall(wall, wallCells(kind, p), p.wall.totalP, p);
   118	  const scr: PreArm = { id: "cP", state: cloneState(wall.state), audit: cloneAudit(wall.audit), records: [] };
   119	  scrambleChannels(scr.state, scr.audit, seed, [CH.P], lowbias32, "scramble-P");
   120	  const out = runPre([wall, scr], p, { allowP: true });
   121	  return { d: out.b!.y - out.cP!.y, wall: out.b!, scrambled: out.cP! };
   122	}
   123	
   124	/** The declared initial budget of a real block at this seed (founders included), without running anything. */
   125	export function nominalBudget(seed: number, p: Protocol = PROTOCOL): Budget {
   126	  const a = openAudit(startWorld(candidateOf(p.panel[0], p), "builder", seed, p));
   127	  return { matterStart: a.matterStart, energyStart: a.energyStart };
   128	}
   129	
   130	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   131	// takeover-only
   132	
   133	/** A receiver that cannot synthesize anything: zero controller weights, no gene bits. */
   134	export const inertReceiver = (p: Protocol = PROTOCOL): Genome => ({ ...founderGenome(founderOf(p.receiverCluster)), weights: new Int8Array(160), genes: 0 });
   135	
   136	/** Non-living detritus B on the halo ring next to the footprint (funded from A there and from E elsewhere); an inert receiver is placed. */
   137	export function takeoverBlock(seed: number, p: Protocol = fixtureProtocol(), detritus = 200): { outcomes: Partial<Record<ArmId, ArmOutcome>>; contrasts: Contrasts } {
   138	  const base = receiverAlone({ audit: uniformBudget(p) }, seed, p);
   139	  const cond: PreArm = { id: "b", state: cloneState(base.state), audit: cloneAudit(base.audit), records: [] };
   140	  const ring = regionCellsExcludingFootprint(p);
   141	  audited("detritus", cond.state, cond.audit, () => {
   142	    const s = cond.state;
   143	    debitEven(s, CH.A, detritus, ring);
   144	    setChannel(s, CH.B, ring, detritus);
   145	    debitEven(s, CH.E, (s.cfg.eB - s.cfg.eA) * detritus, outsideRegion(p));
   146	    return { detritus };
   147	  });
   148	  const outcomes = runPre(controlsAround(cond, base, seed, p), p, { genome: inertReceiver(p) });
   149	  return { outcomes, contrasts: contrastsOf(outcomes) };
   150	}
   151	
   152	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   153	// null field and guards
   154	
   155	export function nullBlock(seed: number, p: Protocol = fixtureProtocol()): { outcomes: Partial<Record<ArmId, ArmOutcome>>; contrasts: Contrasts } {
   156	  const base = receiverAlone({ audit: uniformBudget(p) }, seed, p);
   157	  const cond: PreArm = { id: "b", state: cloneState(base.state), audit: cloneAudit(base.audit), records: [] };
   158	  const outcomes = runPre(controlsAround(cond, base, seed, p), p);
   159	  return { outcomes, contrasts: contrastsOf(outcomes) };
   160	}
   161	
   162	export interface GuardResult {
   163	  id: string;
   164	  ok: boolean;
   165	  detail: string;
   166	}
   167	
   168	/** Guards that must refuse or flag: each returns ok when the bad input was refused as designed. */
   169	export function runGuards(): GuardResult[] {
   170	  const p = fixtureProtocol();
   171	  const out: GuardResult[] = [];
   172	  const attempt = (id: string, fn: () => void, want: RegExp): void => {
   173	    try {
   174	      fn();
   175	      out.push({ id, ok: false, detail: "no refusal" });
   176	    } catch (e) {
   177	      const ok = e instanceof NicheInvalid && want.test(e.reason);
   178	      out.push({ id, ok, detail: e instanceof NicheInvalid ? e.reason : String(e) });
   179	    }
   180	  };
   181	  const fresh = () => receiverAlone({ audit: uniformBudget(p) }, 1, p);
   182	  const words = encodeGenome(founderGenome(founderOf(p.receiverCluster)), 0, packLineageLo(fresh().state.cfg, p.receiverRaw));
   183	  attempt("overwrite-attempt", () => {
   184	    const x = fresh();
   185	    x.state.cells[CH.B * cellCount(x.state.cfg) + footprintCells(p)[0]] = 5;
   186	    x.audit.energyStart += 50n;
   187	    x.audit.matterStart += 5n;
   188	    placeInoculum(x.state, x.audit, footprintCells(p), words);
   189	  }, /refused/);
   190	  attempt("pool-cap", () => {
   191	    const x = fresh();
   192	    const i = footprintCells(p)[0];
   193	    const old = x.state.cells[CH.E * cellCount(x.state.cfg) + i];
   194	    x.state.cells[CH.E * cellCount(x.state.cfg) + i] = 2 ** 28 - 1;
   195	    x.audit.energyStart += BigInt(2 ** 28 - 1 - old);
   196	    placeInoculum(x.state, x.audit, footprintCells(p), words);
   197	  }, /refused/);
   198	  attempt("underfunded-reserve", () => {
   199	    const x = fresh();
   200	    debitEven(x.state, CH.A, 10 ** 9, [0]);
   201	  }, /underfunded/);
   202	  attempt("empty-receiver-reserve", () => {
   203	    const x = fresh();
   204	    placeInoculum(x.state, x.audit, footprintCells(p), words);
   205	    placeInoculum(x.state, cloneAudit(x.audit), footprintCells(p), words);
   206	  }, /refused|no cell|occupied/);
   207	  return out;
   208	}
   209	
   210	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   211	// fixture decisions
   212	
   213	export interface FixtureReport {
   214	  id: string;
   215	  seeds: readonly number[];
   216	  contrasts: Contrasts[];
   217	  naiveK: number;
   218	  naiveWouldSay: "benefit" | "no benefit";
   219	  matchedControls: "caught" | "not caught" | "n/a";
   220	  label: CandidateResult["label"];
   221	  growthAttributed: boolean[];
   222	  synthesis: number[];
   223	}
   224	
   225	const col = (cs: Contrasts[], k: keyof Contrasts): number[] => cs.map((c) => c[k] as number);
   226	
   227	export function decideFixture(id: string, seeds: readonly number[], results: { outcomes: Partial<Record<ArmId, ArmOutcome>>; contrasts: Contrasts }[], delta: number): FixtureReport {
   228	  const cs = results.map((r) => r.contrasts);
   229	  const dec = decideCandidates(
   230	    [
   231	      {
   232	        id,
   233	        status: "ok",
   234	        invalidReasons: [],
   235	        contrasts: { builder: col(cs, "builder"), poly: col(cs, "poly"), space: col(cs, "space"), inoc: col(cs, "inoc"), spaceP: col(cs, "spaceP"), spaceNP: col(cs, "spaceNP") },
   236	        delta,
   237	        survivedBlocks: results.filter((r) => r.outcomes.b?.survived).length,
   238	        blocks: results.length,
   239	        polymerAdequate: true,
   240	        expectedBlocks: results.length,
   241	      },
   242	    ],
   243	    true,
   244	  )[0];
   245	  const naiveK = col(cs, "naive").filter((x) => x > 0).length;
   246	  const naivePositive = naiveK * 2 > results.length && col(cs, "naive").sort((a, b) => a - b)[Math.floor(results.length / 2)] >= delta;
   247	  return {
   248	    id,
   249	    seeds,
   250	    contrasts: cs,
   251	    naiveK,
   252	    naiveWouldSay: naivePositive ? "benefit" : "no benefit",
   253	    matchedControls: naivePositive ? (dec.label === "supported" ? "not caught" : "caught") : "n/a",
   254	    label: dec.label,
   255	    growthAttributed: results.map((r) => !!r.outcomes.b?.growthAttributed),
   256	    synthesis: results.map((r) => r.outcomes.b?.synthesis ?? 0),
   257	  };
   258	}

     1	// Exact, audited transfers between the conditioning stage and the assay stage. Every operation is analytical (outside the step
     2	// function), integer, and checked against the budget invariant: lattice + off-lattice reserve + cumulative analytic heat.
     3	//   energy:  lattice.energy + heatOut + heatAnalytic + reserveEnergy  ==  energyStart + lightIn
     4	//   matter:  lattice.matter + reserveB                                ==  matterStart
     5	// `ledgerResidual` counts neither the reserve nor heatAnalytic, so the wrapper below is the audited quantity.
     6	import { CELL_CHANNELS, CH, G, GENOME_CHANNELS, MOT_ZERO, POOL_MAX, cellCount, totalsOf, validateState, type WorldConfig, type WorldState } from "@bl/schema";
     7	
     8	export class NicheInvalid extends Error {
     9	  reason: string;
    10	  constructor(reason: string) {
    11	    super(reason);
    12	    this.reason = reason;
    13	  }
    14	}
    15	
    16	export interface Audit {
    17	  energyStart: bigint;
    18	  matterStart: bigint;
    19	  /** Receiver reserve still off the lattice: B valued at eB per quantum, E at one. */
    20	  reserveB: number;
    21	  reserveE: number;
    22	  /** Cumulative energy released by analytical conversions, booked as heat. */
    23	  heatAnalytic: bigint;
    24	}
    25	
    26	export const reserveEnergy = (cfg: WorldConfig, a: Audit): bigint => BigInt(cfg.eB) * BigInt(a.reserveB) + BigInt(a.reserveE);
    27	
    28	export function residuals(s: WorldState, a: Audit): { energy: bigint; matter: bigint } {
    29	  const t = totalsOf(s.cfg, s.cells);
    30	  return {
    31	    energy: t.energy + s.heatOut + a.heatAnalytic + reserveEnergy(s.cfg, a) - a.energyStart - s.lightIn,
    32	    matter: t.matter + BigInt(a.reserveB) - a.matterStart,
    33	  };
    34	}
    35	
    36	export function assertAudit(s: WorldState, a: Audit, where: string): void {
    37	  const r = residuals(s, a);
    38	  if (r.energy !== 0n || r.matter !== 0n) throw new NicheInvalid(`${where}: nonzero residual (energy ${r.energy}, matter ${r.matter})`);
    39	}
    40	
    41	export function cloneState(s: WorldState, cfg: WorldConfig = s.cfg): WorldState {
    42	  return { cfg, step: s.step, cells: s.cells.slice(), genome: s.genome.slice(), lightIn: s.lightIn, heatOut: s.heatOut, flux: s.flux.slice() };
    43	}
    44	export const cloneAudit = (a: Audit): Audit => ({ ...a });
